Staff Report December 10, 2024.pdf
162
 Community Development Department 
P.O. Box 128 
60 North Main Street 
Coalville, Utah 84017 
summitcounty.org 
 
  
 
 
STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Ray Milliner, County Planner   
Date of Meeting: December 10, 2024 
Type of Item:  Code Amendment - Public Hearing, Possible Recommendation 
Process:  Legislative Review 
File #:   24-012 
 
 
Recommendation: Staff has reviewed the application for compliance with all standards 
in the Snyderv ille Basin Development Code (Code) and found that it m eets the minimum 
required for approval.  Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning  Commission 
(SBPC) review the proposed development code amendment , and forward a positive 
recommendation to the County Council , pursuant to the findings of fact, and conclusions of 
law found in this S taff Report (the public hearing for this item was opened and closed on June 
25, 2024). 
 
Proposal 
 
The applicant  has applied to amend the Snyderville Basin Development Code to make “Farm 
Stand for Plant and Agricultural Product Sales” a n allowed use in the Community Commercial 
(CC) zone and a conditional use  in the Rural Residential (RR), Service Commercial (SC) and 
Neighborhood Commercial (NC) zones.  
 
Background 
 
The applicant operates a temporary seasonal plant and agricultural  sales use on her property at 
1285 West Old Ranch Road. The farm grows vegetables, flowers, and herbs on approximately 3 
acres during the summer months and grows in an existing 2 ,400 square foot greenhouse during 
the winter months. The vegetables are then sold at an onsite farm stand or distributed to local 
restaurants.  
 
Each year, the applicant submits a temporary use permit application to operate between the 
months of May and October. Over the years, the use has expanded occasionally to include food 
truck sales, and to offer events  such as weddings or corporate events  (events are all reviewed 
and permitted through the County Special Event Department).  
 
163
2 
 
The use is permitted as a “Seasonal Plant and Agricultural Sales” use. The use is listed as a “T” 
or temporary use in the Use Table, and defined as: 
 
“Seasonal Plant and Agricultural Sales  The sale of agricultural or ornamental plants 
during the time of year in which the products are harvested.” 
 
The temporary use permit criteria limit the duration of the use to one year and require that the 
site be returned to its original condition prior to the issue of the permit. The use does operate 
only during summer months, but it has become evident that the use has expanded beyond the 
scope of the temporary use permit criteria.  
 
To remedy the temporary use problem, staff and the applicant began discussing the best way to 
ensure that the use can continue operations without expanding to a point that it has a negative 
effect on surrounding properties. It was determined that a code amendment creating a spot in 
the use table and criteria in chapter 8 would be the best way to allow the use and mitigate 
potential impacts. Consequently, the applicant applied for a code amendment including draft 
language.   
 
 
Highlights of the proposed language: 
 
• Creates a definition of “Farm Stand for Plant and Agricultural Product Sales.” 
• Makes the use a conditional use  in the RR, SC, and NC zones  and an allowed use in the 
CC zone.  
• Creates criteria addressing parking, location, access, and hours of operation. 
• Language allowing Mobile Food Businesses as an accessory to the principal use  was 
removed, the applicant must follow existing rules in the development code.   
 
This application was originally reviewed by the Planning Commission on June 25, 2024. The 
Commission opened and closed a public hearing. Following the public hearing, the Commission 
asked staff and the applicant to work together to tighten up the language to ensure that no 
unintended consequences occur because of this ordinance. Staff and the applicant took a stab 
at amendments to the language and have returned for further discussion with the Commission. 
 
 
Analysis and Findings 
Section 10 -7-3 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code establishes a process for 
amendments to the text of the Code . It states that whenever an amendment to the Code is 
initiated, it must be reviewed by the Planning Commission who will deliver a recommendation 
to the County Council. The County Council, after holding a public hearing, can approve, approve 
with modifications, or deny the amendment subject to the following criteria: 
 
Criteria 1 : The amendment shall be consistent with the goals, objectives, and policies of the 
general plan. 
164
3 
 
 
Analysis: Chapter 2 of the Snyderville Basin General Plan States: 
 
“Policy 2.21: Encourage locally based and neighborhood commercial businesses. 
 
OBJECTIVE H : Preserve the Snyderville Basin’s working farms and ranches, 
remaining unfragmented landscapes.  
 
Policy 2.39: Work with property owners to maintain working farms and ranches 
as a viable industry.” 
 
This amendment will provide the applicant, and other farms that meet the criteria, an 
avenue to stay in business by offering a permanent place to sell their goods and  offer 
limited enhancements such as special events or food trucks  (when allowed as part of a 
special event permit). Because of the urbanization and increase in property values in the 
Basin, it is necessary to provide farmers and ranchers with agricultural tourism type uses 
and opportunities to supplement the traditional agricultural business model.  
 
Criteria 2: The amendment shall not permit the use of land that is not consistent with the uses 
of properties nearby. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The current use of the applicant’s land is agricultural. This amendment will 
enable them and other similar property owners to continue using the land as 
agricultural.  
 
Criteria 3: The amendment will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the proposed 
amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The amendment will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the 
proposed amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. COMPLIES 
 
Criteria 4: The amendment will not permit the removal of the then existing restrictions which 
will unduly affect nearby property. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed language will not remove any regulations that will unduly affect 
property owners in the Basin. It will provide farmers and ranchers with an opportunity 
to enhance their business.  
 
Criteria 5 : The amendment will not grant special favors or circumstances solely for one 
property owner or developer. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: These amendments would be applicable to  all property owners in the Basin 
Planning District.  
165
4 
Criteria 6 : The amendment will promote public  health, safety, and welfare better than the 
existing regulations for which the amendment is intended to change. COMPLIES 
An
alysis: The proposed regulations will create an appropriate process for reviewing and 
approving seasonal farm stand uses. 
Recommendation 
Staff has reviewed the application for compliance with all standards in the Snyderville Basin 
Development Code (Code) and found that it m eets the minimum required for approval. Staff 
recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning  Commission (SBPC) review the proposed 
development code amendment, conduct a  public hearing, and forward a positive 
recommendation to the County Council, pursuant t o the findings of fact, and conclusions of 
law found in this Staff Report. 
F
indings of Fact: 
1. T
he applicant is the owner of the Copper Moose Farm located at 1285 West Old Ranch
Road.
2. She has applied to amend the Snyderville Basin Development Code to make “Farm Stand
for Plant and Agricultural Product Sales” a n allowed use in the Community Commercial
(CC) zone and a conditional  use in the Rural Residential (RR), Service Commercial (SC)
and Neighborhood Commercial (NC) zones.
3. The farm grows vegetables, flowers, and herbs on approximately 3 acres during the
summer months and grows in an existing 2400 square foot greenhouse during the
winter months.
4. The vegetables are then sold at an onsite farm stand or distributed to local restaurants.
5. Each year, the applicant submits a temporary use permit application to operate
between the months of May and October.
6. The use has expanded occasionally to include food truck sales, and to offer events such
as weddings or corporate events (events are all reviewed and permitted through the
County Special Event Department).
7. The use is permitted as a “Seasonal Plant and Agricultural Sales” use. The use is listed as
a “T” or temporary use in the Use Table.
8. The temporary use permit criteria limit the duration of the use to one year and require
that the site be returned to its original condition prior to the issue of the permit.
9. This amendment will provide the applicant, and other farms that meet the criteria, an
avenue to stay in business by offering a permanent place to sell their goods and offer
limited enhancements such as special events or food trucks.
10. The proposed language would create a definition of “Farm Stand for Plant and
Agricultural Product Sales.”
11. The proposed language would create criteria addressing parking, location, access, and
hours of operation.
166
5 
 
12. The proposed language would allow Mobile Food Businesses to be an accessory to the 
principal use when approved as part of the low impact permit.   
 
Conclusions of Law:  
 
1. The amendment is consistent with the goals, objectives, and policies of the General 
Plan. 
2. The amendment does not permit the use of land that is not consistent with the uses of 
properties nearby. 
3. The amendment does not permit suitability of the properties affected by the proposed 
amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. 
4. The amendment will not permit the removal of the existing restrictions which will 
unduly affect nearby property. 
5. The amendment will not grant special favors or circumstances solely for one property 
owner or developer. 
6. The amendment is consistent with the requirements established in chapter 7 of the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code.  
7. The proposed amendment is not detrimental to public health, safety, and welfare. 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments  
Public notice for this item was published in the Park Record on June 10, 2024. The public hearing 
was held on June 25, 2024, public input was taken, and the hearing was closed.

SNYDERVILLE BASIN PLANNING COMMISSION AGENDA ITEM REPORT

December 10,2024 Staff Report.pdf
172
 Community Development Department 
P.O. Box 128 
60 North Main Street 
Coalville, Utah 84017 
summitcounty.org 
 
  
 
 
STAFF REPORT 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Ray Milliner, County Planner 
Date of Meeting: December 10, 2024 
Type of Item:  Code Amendment - Public Hearing, Possible Action 
Process:  Legislative Review 
File #:   24-167 
 
 
RECOMMENDATION: Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission 
review the proposed Development Code amendments, conduct a public hearing and forward a 
positive recommendation to the Summit County Council per the findings of fact and conclusions 
of law in this report.  
 
Proposal 
 
This language would require developers to install electric vehicle charging stations and bicycle 
parking in new developments, create specific criteria for solar arrays, amend language for service 
stations, create definitions for the uses and amend the use table to accommodate the changes.  
 
Background 
 
Over the past few months, planning staff has been working with an internal committee to create 
language in the Development Code that would require developers to install electric vehicle 
charging stations, and bicycle parking in new developments and to clarify regulations for solar 
systems. Committee members included representatives from the County Planning, Sustainability, 
Transportation, and Engineering departments as well as a consultant from Utah Clean Energy, a 
nonprofit who was tasked with helping the County improve its electric vehicle capabilities.  
 
The purpose of the regulations is to compliment the County Council strategic objective for 
environmental stewardship which states: 
 
“The County will demonstrate leadership and set direction for plans, policies, 
partnerships, and initiatives to secure, protect, and conserve our water, land, and 
air quality for the present and future, as well as implement an internal 
organizational culture that supports environmental stewardship.” 
 
 
173
   
 
2 
 
Electric Vehicle Charging Stations 
 
This language would create a new section in Chapter 4-9 Parking Requirements in the Snyderville 
Basin Development Code. Highlights include: 
 
• All new development in the Basin would be subject to the regulations.  
• 10% of parking spaces for multifamily and commercial developments in the NMU, RC, TC, 
CC, NC and SC zones would be required to have class two or class three chargers.  
• 20% of the spaces for multifamily and commercial developments in the NMU, RC, TC, CC, 
NC and SC zones must be EV ready (have the conduit installed but not have the chargers 
themselves).  
• New single -family homes must be built EV ready with either level one or level two 
capabilities.  
• Creates standards for location, maintenance, protection, snow removal, and accessibility.  
 
The purpose of these  regulations is: research provided by County Sustainability staff and Utah 
Clean Energy indicate that EV use will continue to grow, and th erefore, requiring infrastructure 
at the time of construction is much more economical than installing it after construction has been 
completed.   
 
Bicycle Parking 
 
This language would also create a new section in Chapter 4 -9 Parking Requirements in the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code.  Highlights: 
 
• Regulations apply to all multifamily and commercial in the NMU, RC, TC, CC, NC and SC 
zones. 
• Requires 1 short term bike parking space per 1,000 square feet of leasable area up to 20 
spaces and one space for every 5 multifamily units.  
• Requires 1 long-term parking space for every 3 multifamily units and 1 per 10 employees 
in commercial.  
• Requires that 1 of 6 long term spaces be able to accommodate electric bikes.  
• Creates standards for both long- and short-term parking areas.  
 
Requiring long and short -term spaces will take an entire bicycle trip into account: secure long -
term storage at home, safe paths on the road, and secure temporary storage at the 
destination. Bicycle parking also mitigates some of the problems associated with a high number 
of bicycle commuters, such as damage to trees, street signs, and other street furniture from 
impromptu bicycle parking; clutter and blocked sidewalks from bicycles; and other nuisances 
from bicycles strewn around without adequate placement mechanisms.  By making bicycle trips 
more convenient and secure, people are more likely to choose bicycling for daily trips and to treat 
174
   
 
3 
 
the bicycle as a normal, practical mode of transportation rather than a specialty or tool for 
recreation.  
 
Solar Energy Systems 
 
This section would create requirements and criteria for all solar energy systems in Chapter 8 of 
the Snyderville Basin Development Code. Highlights: 
 
• Make all solar energy systems in the Basin allowed uses, provided they meet the 
standards in Chapter 8.  
• Creates standards for Building Integrated, Roof Mounted and Ground Mounted solar 
energy systems.  
 
To date, the Code is silent on solar energy systems, this means that they are prohibited. Creation 
of these rules will legitimize solar structures and help prevent any issues with enforcement of the 
Code. Creation of the rules will enable property owners to reduce electric bills, receive tax credits 
and reduce pressure on the electric grid in a simple and efficient way.  
 
Analysis 
Section 10-7-3 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code states that whenever there is initiated 
an amendment to the Code, it must be reviewed by the Planning Commission who will deliver a 
recommendation to the County Council. The County Council, after holding a public hearing, shall 
approve, approve with modifications or deny the amendment according to the following criteria. 
 
Criteria 1 : The amendment shall be consistent with the goals, objectives, and policies of the 
general plan. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: Objective C of Chapter 5: Sustainability, Cultural and Natural Resources of the 
Snyderville Basin General Plan sates: 
 
“Enhance, promote, and protect a sustainable development framework for the 
future.”  
 
Policy 5.19: Preservation of Air Quality: in the Snyderville Basin General Plan states:  
 
“Ensure that development does not contribute significantly to the degradation of 
air quality and minimizes the impacts of wood burning stoves, automobiles, or 
other similar air quality pollutants.” 
 
Goal 1: Walking + Biking of the Summit County Active Transportation Plan states:   
 
175
   
 
4 
 
“Provide a complete, well- connected, and easily accessible network of trails, 
bicycle lanes, and sidewalks for safe, convenient, and pleasant transportation.” 
 
Each of the proposed additions to the development code will promote the objectives and 
goals of the Snyderville Basin General Plan as well as the Summit County Active 
Transportation Plan.  Requiring EV charging stations will aid in the County efforts to 
promote clean air quality as it will encourage the use of EVs rather than gas automobiles. 
Requiring bike parking will encourage the use of bicycles as a mode of transportation 
rather than just as a recreational or work out tool as it will provide people with a place to 
park the bike both at the beginning and end of their journey. Creating rules and 
regulations for solar energy systems will give property owners the opportunity to install 
systems that will generate electricity  in a clean and sustainable way without relying on 
coal or dam generated electricity.  
 
Criteria 2: The amendment shall not permit the use of land that is not consistent with the uses 
of properties nearby. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: the proposed amendments will enhance the existing uses allowed in the zones. 
They will provide upgrades for existing uses.  
 
Criteria 3: The amendment will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the proposed 
amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis:  The amendment will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the 
proposed amendment to the uses to which they have been restricted.  
 
Criteria 4: The amendment will not permit the removal of the then existing restrictions which will 
unduly affect nearby property. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: The proposed Code language does not remove any existing restrictions that 
would unduly affect nearby property owners. It would add to the existing list of permitted 
uses and provide property owners with opportunities to take advantage of things that 
were not previously available to them.   
 
Criteria 5:  The amendment will not grant special favors or circumstances solely for one property 
owner or developer. COMPLIES 
 
Analysis: These regulations will apply to all property owners in the zones where the use 
is allowed.    
 
Criteria 6: The amendment will promote public health, safety and welfare better than the existing 
regulations for which the amendment is intended to change. COMPLIES 
 
176
   
 
5 
 
Analysis: The proposal is designed to promote the sustainability goals of the Summit 
County Council. Implementation of these regulations will contribute to cleaner air quality, 
promoting alternative transportation goals and the use of clean energy sources.    
 
Recommendation 
 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission review the proposed 
Development Code amendments, conduct a public hearing and forward a positive 
recommendation to the Summit County Council per the findings of fact and conclusions of law 
in this report.  
 
Findings of Fact 
 
1. This language would require developers to install electric vehicle charging stations and 
bicycle parking in new developments, create specific criteria for solar arrays, amend 
language for service stations, create definitions for the uses and amend the use table to 
accommodate the changes.  
2. County Council strategic objective for environmental stewardship which states: 
 
“The County will demonstrate leadership and set direction for plans, policies, 
partnerships, and initiatives to secure, protect, and conserve our water, land, and 
air quality for the present and future, as well as implement an internal 
organizational culture that supports environmental stewardship.” 
 
3. Research provided by County Sustainability staff and Utah Clean Energy indicate that 
Electric Vehicle use will continue to grow in Summit County , and therefore, requiring 
infrastructure for Electric Vehicles at the time of construction is much more economical 
than installing it after construction has been completed.   
4. Requiring long and short -term spaces will take the entire trip into account: secure long -
term storage at home, safe paths on the road, and secure temporary storage at the 
destination.  
5. To date the Code is silent on solar energy systems, this means that they are prohibited. 
Creation of these rules will legitimize solar structures and help prevent any issues with 
enforcement of the code. 
6. The proposed language would add to the existing list of permitted uses and provide 
property owners with opportunities to take advantage of items  that were not previously 
available to them.   
7. Each of the proposed additions to the development code will promote the objectives and 
goals of the Snyderville Basin General Plan as well as the Summit County Active 
Transportation Plan.   
 
 
 
177
   
 
6 
 
Conclusions of Law: 
 
1. The amendment is consistent with the goals, objectives, and policies of the General Plan. 
2. The amendment will not permit the use of land that is not consistent with the uses of 
properties nearby. 
3. The amendment will not permit suitability of the properties affected by the proposed 
amendment for the uses to which they have been restricted. 
4. The amendment will not permit the removal of the existing restrictions which will unduly 
affect nearby property. 
5. The amendment will not grant special favors or circumstances solely for one property 
owner or developer. 
6. The amendment will promote public  health, safety and welfare better than the existing 
regulations for which the amendment is intended to change.

To be added to Chapter 10-4-9: PARKING REQUIREMENTS: 
 
10-4-9.I: Electric Vehicle Parking  
 
1. Purpose: The purpose of these regulations is to: 
 
a. Respond to and/or prepare for the increased need for electric vehicle 
infrastructure resulting from increased ownership and usage of electric vehicles.  
b. Encourage the establishment of convenient, cost- effective electric vehicle 
infrastructure. 
c. Encourage the location of electric vehicle infrastructure, to the extent possible, in 
areas where any potential adverse impacts will be minimized. 
 
2. Applicability:  
 
a. The provisions in this section shall apply to all new Multifamily Development and 
commercial Development in the RC, TC, CC, NC, and SC zones , and all new Single 
Family Attached, Single Family Detached, and Two-Family Dwelling Units.  
 
3. Electric Vehicle Parking Requirements: 
 
a. Electric Vehicle Charging Station Required : All Multifamily and commercial 
Developments in the TC, RC, CC, NC and SC zones  shall provide Electric Vehicle 
Charging Stations subject to the following:  
 
1. A minimum of t en percent (10%) of the Off- Street parking spaces shall 
have an Electric Vehicle Charging Station; and  
2. Electric Vehicle Charging Stations shall count toward the required number 
of parking spaces; and 
3. Electric Vehicle Charging Stations shall be in the same lot as the P rincipal 
Use; and  
4. Electric Vehicle Charging Stations shall be Level Two or Level Three type. 
 
b. Electric Vehicle Ready Required: All new Multifamily and commercial developments 
in the TC, RC, CC, NC and SC zones , and all new Single Family Attached, Single Family 
Detached or Two-Family dwelling units shall be Electric Vehicle Ready  subject to the 
following:  
 
1. A Minimum of twenty percent (20%) of the parking spaces for Multifamily and 
commercial Developments in the TC, RC, CC, NC, and SC zones shall be Electric 
Vehicle Ready with either a Level Two or Level Three charging capability; and 
182
   
 
11 
 
2. Each Single Family Attached, Single Family Detached or Two -Family dwelling 
unit shall provide 1 Electric Vehicle Ready parking space with either Level One 
or Level Two charging capability; and  
3. Electric Vehicle Ready spaces shall be shown on the approved site plan; and 
4. Electric Vehicle Ready spaces s hall count toward the required number of 
parking spaces. 
 
c. Standards. All Electric Vehicle Charging Stations associated with Multifamily or 
Commercial developments shall conform to the following standards: 
 
a. Location. Battery charging station outlets and connector devices shall be no 
less than 15  inches and no higher than 48 inches from the surface where 
mounted. Equipment mounted on pedestals, lighting posts, bollards, or other 
devices shall be designed and located to not impede pedestrian travel or 
create trip hazards on sidewalks or impede ADA standards.  
b. Maintenance. Electric V ehicle Charging Stations shall be maintained in all 
respects including the functioning of the equipment. A phone number or other 
contact information shall be provided on the equipment for reporting when it 
is not functioning, or other problems are encountered.  
c. Protection. Adequate Electric Vehicle C harging Station protection, such as 
concrete-filled steel bollards shall be used. Curbing may be used in lieu of 
bollards, if the battery charging station is set back a minimum of 24 inches 
from the face of the curb.  
d. Snow Removal. An applicant shall install electric vehicle charging stations that 
are safe for use in inclement weather. Cords, cables, and connectors shall be 
stored at least 24 inches above the ground. Property owners,  or those 
responsible for the operation and maintenance of the charging station shall 
manage cords so that they do not impede snow removal and shall remove 
snow from electric vehicle charging stations. 
e. Accessibility. Under the ADA and ABA Accessibility Standards, EV charging 
stations must comply with the technical requirements for floor and ground 
surfaces, clear floor or ground space, reach ranges, operable parts, accessible 
routes, and all other applicable standards found in the International Building 
Code.  
 
10-4-9.J: Bicycle Parking Requirements 
 
1. Applicability:  
 
a. The provisions in this Section apply to all new Single Family Attached, Multifamily 
and commercial Development in the TC, RC, CC, SC, and NC zones. 
 
183
   
 
12 
 
2. Minimum Required Bicycle Parking Spaces : Unless otherwise exempted by an existing 
development agreement, or consent agreement:  
 
a. Short-term Bicycle Parking spaces shall be provided at a rate of one (1) space per 
one thousand (1,000) square feet of net leasable area up to a maximum of 20 
spaces for commercial development and one space for every 5 units in Multifamily 
developments. Notwithstanding, a minimum of three (3) spaces shall be provided 
for all applicable uses. 
b. Long-term Bicycle Parking shall be provided at a rate of one (1) space per three (3) 
dwelling units for Multi -family Dwellings and one space per ten (10) employees 
generated for uses as outlined in Chapter 10-5-6 of this Title.  
c. Short and Long -term bicycle parking areas shall have at least two (2) oversized 
bicycle parking spaces to accommodate large bicycles such as cargo, recumbent, 
tandem, electric, and bicycles with trailers. 
d. One (1) of every 6 spaces in all bicycle parking areas shall be Electric Vehicle Ready.  
 
3. Exceptions:  
 
a. The Planning Commission may reduce or modify the required outdoor bicycle 
parking and enclosed bicycle storage when the Applicant demonstrates a plan that 
achieves secure bicycle parking facilities that best suits the Uses and physical 
constraints of a Site. 
 
4. Standards 
 
a. Short-term bicycle parking: Unless modified by the Planning Commission, required 
short term parking will meet the following standards: 
 
1. Design and shape of racks shall be reviewed and approved by the County 
Transportation Director; and 
2. Racks shall be located so that bicycles can be attached without impeding 
pedestrian circulation; and 
3. Racks shall be mounted in concrete; and 
4. Racks shall be resistant to rust, corrosion, hammers, and saws; and 
5. Racks shall be large enough allow both the bicycle frame and wheel to 
be locked with a U-lock; and 
6. The Parking area shall be designed to prevent damage to the bicycle and 
to facilitate easy and secure bicycle parking without interference from or 
to adjacent bicycles; and 
7. Short-term bicycle parking areas shall be installed no more than fifty feet 
(50’) from the primary entrance; and 
184
   
 
13 
 
8. Short-term bicycle parking areas shall be in  convenient, highly visible, 
active, areas that are easily accessible from the Right -of-Way, bicycle 
lanes, and/or trails and pathways; and 
9. Short-term bicycle parking areas shall be protected from vehicles; and 
 
b. Long-term bicycle parking. Required long term parking will meet the following 
standards: 
 
1. Long term bicycle parking must be located within the parking area or 
Building for which it is required. 
a. Exception: Long term bicycle parking may be located within a 
garage or accessory building for the use, provided said structure is 
within 250 feet of the use it is intended to serve. 
2. Long term bicycle parking must  either be enclosed or sheltered from the 
weather; and 
3. Long term bicycle parking must be securely locked; and 
4. Contain racks for support and locking of individual bicycles; and 
5. If not located on the ground floor, long term bicycle parking must be clearly 
accessible by ramp, parking area, or  an elevator with interior dimensions 
of at least eighty inches (80”) by fifty-four inches (54”); and 
6. One (1) of every six (6) Long Term Bicycle Parking spaces shall be Electric 
Vehicle Ready.  
 
To be added to Chapter 10-8-8:  
SERVICE STATIONS GASOLINE SERVICE STATION WITH OR WITHOUT A CONVENIENCE STORE: 
 
The purpose of this section is to prescribe standards for service stations in order to limit the 
potential for impairment of traffic flow on roads providing access to such uses; to minimize fire 
hazards and the contamination of ground water resulting from the over concentration of such 
uses within defined areas; to ensure adequate off-street queuing and ingress/egress; and to limit 
the noise and other negative impacts on adjacent land uses. 
A. Separation: No service station shall be located within two hundred feet (200') of a 
single-family dwelling or within five hundred feet (500') of another service station. 
B. Distance To Intersection, Road: Not more than one service station may be located 
within two hundred feet (200') of the intersection of any major road, arterial road or 
collector road with another major road, arterial road or collector road. 
C. Screening: An opaque screening treatment shall be provided on all sides of the 
property that are located adjacent to residentially zoned and residentially used 
property.  
D. Electric Vehicle Charging Stations: Operators may provide  Public Electric Vehicle 
Charging Stations on the property provided they meet the requirements in Chapter 
10-8-19 of this Title.  
 
185
   
 
14 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
To be added to Chapter 10-8 
 
10-8-19: SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEMS  
1. Applicability: 
 
a. Solar energy systems are an allowed accessory use in all zoning districts.  
b. Solar Energy Systems shall not count toward Accessory Building limitations in 
chapter 8-17 of this Title. 
c. Solar Energy Systems must all comply with all applicable standards in this 
section:  
 
2. Solar Energy System, Building Integrated: 
 
a. The solar panels and equipment shall be integrated into the building (walls, 
windows, photovoltaic louvres, roof tiles, etc.). 
b. All associated necessary equipment such as wires and batteries, etc. shall be 
screened from view. 
 
3. Solar Energy System, Roof Mounted: 
 
a. The collector surface and mounting devices for Roof Mounted Solar Energy 
Systems shall not extend beyond the exterior perimeter of the building on which 
the system is mounted or built.  
b. Roof Mounted Solar Energy Systems on pitched roofs shall have the same 
finished pitch as the roof and be no more than ten inches (10”) above the roof.  
c. All Roof Mounted Solar Energy Systems shall comply with the height limitations 
in the zoning district in which they are located. 
 
i. EXCEPTION: On a flat-roofed structure, solar panels may extend up to 
five (5) feet above the zone height, but no more than ten feet (10’) above 
the roof surface and are exempt from any rooftop equipment or 
mechanical system screening requirements.  
 
4. Solar Energy System, Ground Mounted:  
186
   
 
15 
 
 
a. Ground Mounted Solar Energy Systems total collector area shall not exceed 
1,000 square feet.  
b. Ground Mounted Solar Energy Systems shall not exceed fifteen feet (15’) in 
height at maximum tilt, above existing grade.   
c. Ground Mounted Solar Energy are prohibited in front yards, between the 
principal building and the public street.     
d. Ground Mounted Solar Energy Systems shall not extend into the side or rear 
setback when oriented at minimum design tilt.  
e. All Solar Area utility lines shall be placed underground to the extent feasible. 
f. Ground Mounted Solar Energy Systems shall be designed and located to ensure 
solar access without reliance on adjacent properties.

To be added to Chapter 10-11: DEFINITIONS  
 
187
   
 
16 
 
BICYCLE PARKING, SHORT -TERM. Short-term bicycle parking is an area where bicycles may be 
securely stored, with both wheels resting on a stable surface and conveniently accessed and 
removed without requiring the movement of other bicycles, vehicles, or other objects. 
 
BICYCLE PARKING, LONG-TERM. Long-term bicycle storage is a secure, area for the storing and 
locking of bicycles either during work hours or for storage at home. 
 
ELECTRIC VEHICLE (EV) . Any motor vehicle registered to operate on public roadways that 
operates either partially or exclusively on electric energy. 
 
ELECTRIC VEHICLE CHARGING STATION. A power supply device that supplies electrical power for 
recharging plug-in electric vehicles or an electric bicycle. Electric Vehicle Charging Stations can 
be one of the following: 
 
I. Level One: A Level One charging station uses a 120-volt outlet and can provide 
up to 1.9 kilowatts of power to a vehicle  or bicycle, which is enough for slow 
overnight charging. Level One stations are commonly found in Single Family 
Attached, Single Family Detached, or Two-Family dwellings. 
 
II. Level Two: A Level Two charging station runs on 208 -to-240-volt power to 
provide faster charging  and can provide 3.3 to 19.2 kilowatts of power to a 
vehicle. Level Two stations are commonly found at workplaces, shopping 
centers, and other public locations.  
 
III. Level Three: A Level Three charging station provides the fastest charging of 50 
or more kilowatts of power to a vehicle. Level Three charging stations are 
commonly found along heavy traffic corridors and for commercial fleet 
vehicles.  
 
ELECTRIC VEHICLE CHARGING STATION, PRIVATE : An Electric Vehicle Charging Station that is 
privately owned with restricted access (i.e., residential uses, or employee parking lots).    
 
ELECTRIC VEHICLE CHARGING STATION, PUBLIC : An Electric Vehicle Charging Station that is 
publicly owned and publicly available (i.e., park and ride lot, public library parking lot, on -street 
parking) or privately owned and available to visitors of the use (i.e., shopping center parking, gas 
station etc.).   
 
ELECTRIC VEHICLE READY: Development designed and constructed to include a fully wired circuit 
for an Electric Vehicle Charging Station power, including conduit and wiring with the electrical 
service capacity necessary to serve the power outlets to allow for the future installation of Level 
One or Level Two Electric Vehicle Charging Stations, or in the case of electric bicycle parking 
spaces to allow for bicycle charging. 
 
188
   
 
17 
 
GASOLINE SERVICE STATION WITH OR WITHOUT A CONVENIENCE STORE : A place where 
gasoline, motor oil, lubricants, or other minor accessories are retailed directly to the public on 
the premises in combination with the retailing of items typically found in a convenience market 
or supermarket and may include Electric Vehicle Charging Stations. 
 
SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEM, BUILDING INTEGRATED:  A combination of Solar Panels and Solar 
Energy Equipment integrated into any building envelope system, including but not limited to, 
vertical facades, semitransparent skylight systems, solar collectors mounted on the sides of 
buildings serving as awnings, roofing materials, or shading over windows, which produce 
electricity for onsite or offsite consumption.  
SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEM, GROUND MOUNTED: A
  Solar Energy System that is anchored to the 
ground via a pole or other mounting system, detached from any other structure, that generates 
electricity for onsite or offsite consumption.  
SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEM, ROOF MOUNTED:
   A Solar Energy System located on the roof of any 
legally permitted building or structure that produces electricity for onsite or offsite 
consumption.

yderville Basin Planning Commission

ommendation: Staff recommends that the Planning Commission review the proposed 
condominium plat, conduct a public hearing, and forward a positive recommendation to the

staff report.   
P
roject Description 
P
roject Name: Swaner Condominium Plat 
Applicant(s): Sumner Swaner 
Property Representative: Sumner Swaner 
Zone District:  Rural Residential (RR) 
Parcel Number and Size: Lots A and B of the Trout Creek Townhouses 
Type of Process: Administrative 
Final Land Use Authority: Summit County Manager 
P
roposal 
T
he applicant, Sumner Swaner, is requesting approval of a condominium plat for lots A and B of 
the Trout Creek Townhomes. The purpose of the plat is to enable a separate driveway for the 
structure on Lot B and the construction of a garage. No increase in density is proposed.  
191
2 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Background 
 
The applicant, Sumner Swaner, is the owner of Lots A (proposed Unit 1) and B (proposed unit 2) 
of the Trout Creek Townhomes. Both Lots A and B have single family homes in them. The 
applicant would like overlay a two-unit condominium plat on the Lots. Access to the property 
would come from Glenwild Drive via a separate driveway for each home (there is an existing 
driveway for the home on proposed unit 2 and a driveway for the proposed unit 1 is proposed 
as part of this application). The total area of the project would be 1.04 acres.  No increase in 
density is proposed. 
 
The driveway access/easement to the Trout Creek condo units runs through proposed unit 1 
with a six-space parking area on the north side and a three-space parking area on the south 
side. The easement would remain as currently configurated. The parking area on the north side 
is for the benefit of Trout Creek residents and the parking area on the south side is for Trout 
Creek residents and residents of Unit 1.  
 
The purpose of the request is to clean up parking issues between the owner of the lots and the 
owners of the townhomes, and enable him to create a parking area for the home on lot A.  This 
cannot be done under the current configuration of the lots because the additions would not 
meet the required setbacks in the development code.  
192
3 
 
 
Analysis and Findings 
 
Section 10-3-8 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code states, the Commission shall review 
the application and staff report and make a recommendation to the county manager for 
approval, approval with conditions, or denial after a public hearing, then the county manager 
shall review the application, staff report, and commission findings and recommendations and 
thereafter approve, approve with conditions, or deny the proposal. 
 
Staff’s review of the application based on the information presented indicates the project 
complies with the requirements in Chapter 4 of the Snyderville Basin Development Code.

found that it meets the minimum required for

COMPLIES 
18. Architectural 
Regulations for All 
Structures 
The project meets this criterion  COMPLIES 
19. Landscape 
Regulations 
No Changes proposed.  COMPLIES 
20. Lighting Regulations Upon submittal for a building permit the 
applicant will be required to bring all lighting on 
the structure into compliance with Chapter 4-22 
of the Code. This will be reviewed by staff at 
that time.   
COMPLIES  
21. Height Regulations Meets the minimum standard   COMPLIES 
 
Recommendation 
 
Staff recommends that the Planning Commission review the proposed condominium plat, 
conduct a public hearing, and forward a positive recommendation to the County Manager per 
the findings of fact, conclusions of law and conditions of approval in this staff report.   
 
Findings of Fact: 
 
1. The applicant, Sumner Swaner, is the owner of Lots A and B of the Trout Creek 
Townhomes.  
2. The property is in the Rural Residential (RR) zone.  
3. Both Lots A and B have single family homes in them.  
4. The applicant would like to create his own two-unit condominium/subdivision plat on 
Lots A and B of the Trout Creek Townhomes. 
5. Access to the property would come from Glenwild Drive via a separate driveway for 
each home (there is an existing driveway for the home on proposed unit 2 and a 
driveway for the proposed unit 1 is proposed as part of this application).  
194
5 
 
6. The total area of the condo plat would be 1.04 acres.   
7. The driveway access/easement to the Trout Creek condo units runs through proposed 
unit 1 with a six-space parking area on the north side and a three-space parking area on 
the south side.  
8. The access and parking easements would remain as currently configurated.  
9. The parking area on the north side is for the benefit of Trout Creek residents and the 
parking area on the south side is for Trout Creek residents and residents of Unit 1.  
10. The applicant is proposing to create an area for the expansion of the home on proposed 
unit 1 as well as create driveway access for proposed unit 1.   
11. Access to proposed Unit 2 and the Trout Creek Townhomes will remain as is.
 
12. There will be no increase in density because of this condominium plat.   
  
Conclusions of Law: 
1. The proposed Condominium Conversion as conditioned complies with all requirements 
of the Development Code.  
2. There is good cause for the proposed Condominium Conversion.  
3. No one will be materially harmed by this Condominium Conversion.  
4. The use as conditioned is consistent with the General Plan, as amended.  
 
Conditions of Approval: 
 
1. If the plat is not recorded with the County Recorder’s Office within one year of the date 
of approval, this approval shall expire.
